  - Past the 16 Mi-unit output cap the sentinel is dropped too, so the command runs into its 120 s timeout and reports `timedOut: true`.
  - After the shell exits (`!exit`) or fails to spawn, the queue isn't drained, so a command queued behind it waits for the next command to finish.
  - Not mirrored: TS never answers commands still queued at `dispose()` (session end). Rust answers them `BashSession destroyed`, as for a call after the dispose, so the server's graceful shutdown never waits on them.
- Scheduled loops (S11a): the tracker dedupes a CronCreate only while it's pending, so a re-observed one (a JSONL resync record with a uuid the live stream never carried) goes pending again, and its replayed result re-promotes it in place with the replay's `startedAt` and `cancelled: false`. A re-observed superseded wake-up is armed again over the live one. In both cases the records that follow in the same resync usually restore the state.
- `mcp-dynamic` (S15a): the SDK's `setMcpServers` disconnects every in-process server missing from the new set, including the session's own `claudius_goal`, so `report_goal_achieved` is gone for the rest of the session after any call.
- A re-broadcast TaskCreate `tool_use` duplicates its to-do. The "already promoted" dedupe compares the temp `tool_use_id` with ids that have since become the real task id, so it never matches. `resyncFromDisk` dedupes by uuid alone, and the JSONL's split content-block records carry uuids the live stream never sent, so the old TaskCreate and its result can replay. Seen in the TS app on 2026-10-26: the mid-turn reminder listed the last-created item several times.
- `fs/dirs` (D04):
  - Containment is lexical and `stat` / `readdir` / `mkdir` follow symlinks, so a symlink inside the root lists, and creates folders in, a directory outside it.
  - The TCC gate and the container 403 compare exact strings. On a case-insensitive disk (the APFS default), `?path=desktop` or `Library/containers` passes both and reads the folder without the heads-up.
  - POST has no TCC or container check: `{parent: "Desktop"}` creates there without `ack`.
  - With `CLAUDIUS_FS_ROOT=/`, nothing below `/` is reachable (`startsWith("//")`): every other listing and every create is a 403.
  - A folder named `..foo` inside the root can't be a POST parent (a 403 from the `relative(…).startsWith("..")` check).
- `assertWithin` (I14d): a base that resolves to `/` refuses every child (`startsWith("//")`), so a workspace at `/`, or a default trusted root of `/`, can't read or write its project/local settings or `.mcp.json`. `GET /api/mcp` and the settings readers throw `PathInjectionError: path escapes base directory`, as TS does.
- `fs/list` (D04): a directory never gets the basename bonus; its `relPath` ends in `/`, so its basename is empty.
- Memory and rules (G08):
  - Containment is lexical and reads, edits and listings follow symlinks. A symlink inside the memory or rules dir (or the dir itself being one) is read, written through by `PATCH`, and listed with its target's size and mtime. A create never goes through one (exclusive, 409) and a delete removes only the link.
  - An index that can't be read (`MEMORY.md` a directory) throws after the memory file was written, patched or unlinked: an empty 500, the change kept, no reminder. Only the 413 rolls back.
  - A cwd whose encoded memory-dir name passes 255 bytes is an empty 500 (`ENAMETOOLONG`); TS never shortens or hashes it.
- Workspace relocation (G09):
  - A sidecar-merge or stamp failure leaves the transcript already renamed into the new folder but reports it in `failed` (the TS type says failed transcripts stay behind), so a retry hits "already exists".
  - The stamp is appended through a symlinked `<id>.jsonl` to wherever it points (the containment check is lexical).
  - `NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%'` also skips user tables like `sqliteXfoo` (neither merged nor re-keyed).
  - A dotless `ınteger` PK passes the JS `toUpperCase() === "INTEGER"` test but isn't a rowid alias, so the source rows land with NULL keys.
  - A source row missing a NOT NULL column the destination has is dropped silently (`INSERT OR IGNORE`).
- CC 2.1.309 session set (R05):
  - A truthy non-string `sessions.title` (a number or BLOB from a foreign writer) makes `isHistoryWorthyDbRow` throw. `/api/sessions/all` is a 500 `{"error":"row.title.trim is not a function"}`, and the move-sessions count silently drops that row and every later one.
  - `retitleIndexedSession`'s read-only probe is outside its `try`. A workspace DB without a `sessions` table (or a `null` store entry) throws after earlier workspaces' rows were updated. The rename route reads that as 0, so with no transcript it answers 500 although the title landed.
- Git (D06):
  - The branch header regex fails on a name with `.` or JS whitespace (`v1.2`, NBSP), so the status reads as detached with a `head` sha. A trailing BOM is trimmed off the branch.
  - `getRepoRoot` trims a top level that ends in whitespace, so git then runs in another dir (`spawn git ENOENT`, or a trimmed twin repo).
  - Untracked (`--no-index`) diffs read any path, absolute or `../` outside the repo, and treat "Could not access" (exit 1) as an empty diff.
  - No `--no-ext-diff`: a repo's `diff.external` runs.
- Git branches (D07):
  - git 2.54 shortens `refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD` to `<remote>`, so `listBranches`' `<remote>/HEAD` filter never fires: `origin` is listed as a remote branch, and checking it out is `unknown branch: origin`. `origin/HEAD` derives the local name `HEAD`, which git refuses.
  - Ambiguous short names (`heads/dup` for a branch named like a tag, `remotes/rem/main` next to a local `rem/main`) are listed that way and can't be checked out (`unknown branch`).
  - A branch whose name starts with `-` or holds JS whitespace (NBSP) is listed, but every route refuses it as `invalid branch name`. A NEL passes JS `\s` and reaches git.
  - `diff <branch>` has no `--`: a name that is only a path diffs that path, one that is both is git's ambiguity error. No `--no-ext-diff`, so `diff.external` runs.
  - Remote delete splits at the first `/`: a remote with a slash in its name is misparsed, and `./x` pushes to the repo itself and deletes its local `x`.
  - Checkout's `refExists` probes swallow every failure, so a probe killed at 15 s or a corrupt packed-refs reads as `unknown branch`.
- Git commit (D08):
  - The `..` check is prefix-only: `./../x` passes, and git refuses it with a 500.
  - Remove unlinks a nested repo's `sub/` directory: `EPERM` on macOS.
  - `commit-message` has no NUL check, so a NUL path is Node's `args[N]` TypeError as a 500.
  - Stage and unstage silently skip paths that aren't in status.
- `sessions/search` (D20):
  - It reads `homedir()/.claude/projects`, not `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and encodes `dir` with `encodeProjectDir` only (not the SDK's hashed name for long paths), so a profile's transcripts and a long cwd's folder aren't searched (a 300-char dir is ENAMETOOLONG → `[]`).
  - The encoding collides: `/tmp/😀 x` and `/tmp/😁 x` share a folder, as do `../../etc` and `.:/.\-etc`.
  - Containment is lexical, so a symlinked `<id>.jsonl` is read wherever it points.
  - Matching runs on the untrimmed `q` over the raw JSONL, keys included (`"type":"user"` matches every file).
  - The snippet unescape runs in pass order, so a raw `\\n` prints as `\ `.
  - Invalid-name files take slots before the 600 cut.
  - A FIFO named `<id>.jsonl` blocks the request forever (in Rust, one blocking-pool thread).
- `sessions/peer-source` (G11):
  - It reads `homedir()/.claude`, not `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and never a profile's `projects/`: a sender whose CLI writes elsewhere is found only through a profile's registry record.
  - The raw `includes` prefilters run before the structural match: an uppercase msgId passes the UUID check but never matches, and an escaped `msg_id`, tool name or `cwd` key in a transcript line is missed.
  - The 200-unit snippet cut can end in a lone high surrogate, which then prefixes a different astral character's body.
  - An empty workspace `rootPath` matches every absolute cwd (`"" + "/"`).
  - A FIFO named `*.jsonl` in the window blocks the request forever (by inspection; in Rust, one blocking-pool thread).
- Voice (D25): a response that isn't a WebSocket upgrade (a 401, a redirect) leaves the session CONNECTING, because TS swallows `unexpected-response`. No error reaches the composer; chunks answer 410 and `close` answers 204 until the client leaves or the 30 s idle reaper fires.
- Voice (D25): the idle reaper counts only chunks and finalize, so a session that only receives transcripts for 30 s is reaped. The upstream handlers close by id, so a late event from a closed session's socket can close a newer session that reuses the id (the renderer draws a fresh uuid each time).
- Settings import (D11):
  - `GET /api/settings/import/:id` runs the worker, so it can commit the system merge.
  - A `__proto__` workspace id's decision is never stored (the prototype setter), so that import re-pauses forever.
  - `mergeDeep` drops a `__proto__` key the target lacks, and a `null` under an inherited `Object.prototype` name.
  - An array `content` is left half-written when `Buffer.from` refuses a chunk.
- Cost (D18b):
  - A `.claudius-cost-cache.json` whose `files` is a truthy primitive or a non-empty string makes `/api/cost` and the overview a 500 until the file is deleted.
  - An array `files` cache is rewritten as nulls and never caches, so every request re-parses every transcript.
  - One turn with a non-string `message.model` (or a token field with its own `toString`) fails the whole report and the overview with a 500.

**SDK host (`claudius-agent`)**
- argv, env, `initialize` and stdin match SDK 0.3.312 byte for byte across 4 representative cases. The golden wire allowlist has been **empty** since A03 (2026-10-26).
  - `initialize` includes `sdkMcpServerConfigs` and the pre-captured `sdkMcpServerManifests` (A02).
  - The sandbox → `--settings` merge (`CZ()`) also matches the SDK's own code on 45 recorded edge cases (A03).
- Sandbox merge: a `network.deniedDomains` that's neither an array, a string nor an object with a numeric `length` counts as empty, where JS would coerce its `.length`. The CLI schema rejects such values anyway.
- Env rules not mirrored, each with its reason in `tests/sdk_snapshot.rs`: `CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS` (the host-side run-end ceiling), the `*_AUTH_REFRESH` flags, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from the internal `resumeConfigDir`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SDK_READS_STARTUP_MARKS` (set only under `startup()` / `prewarm()`'s internal `readsStartupMarks`), and OpenTelemetry `TRACEPARENT`/`TRACESTATE`. The SDK also writes `CLAUDE_AGENT_SDK_VERSION` into its own `process.env`; Rust only puts it in the CLI's env. It also sets `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath=1` on its own `process.env` when it loads (only Windows reads it), so every CLI it spawns with the inherited env has it; Rust doesn't set it (listed in G06's probe wire test).
- Not modelled, because Claudius never sets them: the initialize fields `toolAliases`, `codeRunningMcpTools`, `webSearchIsolationExemptMcpServers`, `supportedDialogKinds` / `onUserDialog`, `rapidFollowupPreempt`, `forwardNestedSubagentToolCalls`, `workspaceTrust`, and `pluginDelivery: "initialize"`. Use `Options::initialize_extra` if one is ever needed. Nor are an SDK MCP config's per-tool `permission_policy` and `connectorId` (0.3.312), which `createSdkMcpServer` never sets.
- Control requests at and after cleanup match the SDK on 21 recorded cases (A12, `tests/cleanup_golden.rs`):
  - after the reader ended, "Query closed before response received";
  - in flight when the CLI goes (`initialize` included), the exit / error-result error, or that same message on a clean exit;
  - after the exit but before the reader ended, "ProcessTransport is not ready for writing";
  - a write to an ended stdin, or into a dead pipe, is dropped and its request waits for the cleanup;
  - after `close()` (the session's abort), "Operation aborted", and the stream now ends with it too (the session ignores errors once aborted, as TS does); "Claude Code process aborted by user" if the CLI still runs at the SIGTERM 2 s later (R13).

  Remaining differences:
  - On `close()`, Rust fails waiters at once; the SDK fails them at its cleanup, with the same error. This only matters for a reply already on its way.
  - The SDK words an abort "Claude Code process aborted by user" if the CLI's exit is seen before its stdout ends. That never happened in 20 recorded aborts (4 cases × 5 runs).
  - A request after the cleanup, with a CLI that closed stdout but keeps running, is untested.
  - Close timing (R13) matches the SDK on 16 recorded cases. Rust's `close()` is the abort (what `Session::end()` does), so after it the stream ends with the abort's error, where the SDK's own `Query.close()` ends it cleanly at the same time. No `lib/server` caller uses `Query.close()`. The SDK's forward sends two SIGTERMs in one tick (its timer, then Node's `signal`); Rust sends one.
  - Once the server is exiting (`begin_exit`), a closed CLI gets SIGTERM at once and SIGKILL 2 s later, and the shutdown waits for it. A TS host SIGTERMs its CLIs as Node exits and never SIGKILLs, so a CLI that ignores SIGTERM outlives it. A query nobody closed (an in-flight one-shot) isn't SIGTERMed at exit; `kill_on_drop` SIGKILLs it when the runtime drops.
  - `Session::end()` waits for the CLI (at most 5 s), where the TS `end()` returns at once. Ending a session whose CLI ignores EOF (mid-turn) takes the 2 s grace. A CLI that also ignores SIGTERM is SIGKILLed 7 s after the close, after `end()` gave up.
  - After the reader throws (R12) at a CLI that ignores SIGTERM, `oneshot` and the model probe can wait up to 2 s longer than TS: `finish()` runs after the error, and a `for await` that throws doesn't call `return()`. Windows (no SIGTERM; SIGKILL and the forward 7 s after the close) is type-checked only.
- Manifest capture doesn't drop a `toolsListResult` on a mid-capture `tools/list_changed`, because `SdkMcpServer` has no server-initiated messages.
- CLI-initiated control requests match the SDK on 80 recorded requests × 2 setups (A07, `tests/control_golden.rs`), and prompt redelivery on 25 recorded cases (47 runs; R12). Remaining gaps:
  - The SDK's MCP server zod-validates request params (`initialize`, `tools/call`) and tool arguments. It replies -32603 with the zod issue list, or an `isError` "Input validation error…" result. `ToolServer` passes arguments through, so Rust tool handlers must validate their own input. The test's `GAPS` lists these 4 cases.
  - Callback arguments are typed. Where the SDK hands a callback the raw value (a non-string `title`, `tool_use_id`, elicitation `message`…), Rust drops it; `raw` still has the request. A missing `input` reaches `canUseTool` as `{}`, not `undefined`.
  - `PermissionResult.decisionClassification` isn't modelled.
  - No `onUserDialog`, `getOAuthToken`, `getHostAuthToken`, `refreshWorkSecret` or `uiHost`, so their subtypes get the SDK's no-callback replies (silent, or an error).
  - A `control_request` without a string `request_id` is dropped. The SDK would reply with no id.
  - Prompt redelivery (R12): a redelivered entry without a string `request_id` is dropped, like a live one (`REDELIVERY_GAPS`: `odd_request_ids`). When the lists make the SDK's reader throw, the SDK still answers the callbacks it started that settle within microtasks; Rust answers those that settle on their first poll (the same on every recorded case).
- Single-turn mode (`Query::spawn_prompt`, `oneshot`) and the run-end state machine match the SDK on 19 recorded cases and its ceiling-env parser on 52 values (A08). Not ported: the SDK closing stdin after a *finite* streamed input once the run ends (`streamInput` + `waitForRunEnd`). Rust's streaming API has no end-of-input signal, and Claudius aborts when it ends input anyway.
- The reader (`readMessages`) and the transport's stream handling match the SDK on 45 recorded cases (A10, `tests/reader_golden.rs`). That covers the `sdk_host_only` drop, the "Claude Code returned an error result" swap, `commands_changed` → `supported_commands()`, lossy UTF-8 decoding, `readline` line splitting, the UTF-16 stderr tail and Node's signal names. Remaining gaps:
  - Stderr is redacted like the SDK's `up()` (A11, `claudius_agent::redact`, 1610 recorded inputs). One deliberate difference: if a pattern hits `fancy-regex`'s backtracking limit, the whole text becomes `<redacted>`, where V8 would just run longer.
  - Rust is lenient where the SDK's reader throws and ends the stream: on a `result` whose `errors` isn't an array or holds a non-string, and on a `null` message line. The CLI never sends these.
  - The unmatched `control_response` stash isn't kept (`[-]` in A10): only the SDK's internal `awaitControlResponse` reads it.
  - `supported_commands()` returns `[]` when `initialize` had no `commands`; the SDK returns `undefined`.
  - Lines are read with `js::parse_line` (I17): a lone-surrogate escape is U+FFFD where the SDK keeps the lone surrogate (TS re-writes it as `\udXXX`, e.g. on SSE or in a `can_use_tool` reply's `updatedInput`; Rust writes U+FFFD's bytes). A number past f64 is `null` (SDK: ±Infinity); a line nested past 127 levels is still dropped.
- Finding and launching the binary matches the SDK (A09, `tests/binary_golden.rs`): `xZ`'s package lookup on 320 recorded cases, the not-found message, JS entries through `node` with `executableArgs`, and the launch-error wording on 11 recorded failures. Remaining gaps:
  - Port-only: `$CLAUDIUS_CLAUDE_BIN` and `claude` on `PATH` as fallbacks, and the `node_modules` walk starts from the session cwd, the process cwd and the executable's directory, not from an SDK install. On Windows the `PATH` fallback only looks for `claude.exe` (no `PATHEXT`; an npm `claude.cmd` shim can't be spawned without a shell anyway).
  - A file the kernel can't execute (ENOEXEC, e.g. a script without a shebang) fails in Node with "spawn ENOEXEC", but std's `execvp` retries it through `/bin/sh`.
  - The interpreter for a JS entry defaults to `node`; the SDK picks `bun` when it runs under bun.
  - Windows launch errors only map not-found (ENOENT) and permission (EPERM); libuv's full Win32 table isn't ported. The EAGAIN/EMFILE/ENFILE wording isn't exercised by a recorded case.
  - Windows, Linux and Android builds are type-checked (clippy), not run.
- Spawn hygiene: CLI children close inherited non-close-on-exec descriptors (`close_leaked_fds`). macOS `std` can leak a concurrently created pipe into them, which would keep a sibling CLI's stdin open. The bash-mode shell (S10) does the same; any other long-lived child the port adds must too. Short-lived ones (`git`, `reg.exe`) only delay.
- Fork safety (I35, Rust only): a `pre_exec` hook makes the spawn a `fork`, and on macOS a fork while another thread makes libnotify's globals kills the child before it runs ("os_once_t is corrupt"). Every fork-path spawn calls `claudius_agent::prepare_fork()` first: the CLI, `shell.rs`, `bash_mode.rs`, `reveal.rs` and `updater/restart.rs`. A new one must too. `tests/fork_safety.rs` holds the race at 0 deaths in 100 fresh processes. Node spawns with `posix_spawn`, so TS never had it.

**Settings files (`settings.rs`)**
- At parity on the recorded cases (D10a). Differences:
  - A lone surrogate in a reason (V8's token is half an astral character) is dropped (2 recorded).
  - A lone-surrogate escape in a value, or a string file spread through an astral character, becomes U+FFFD, where TS keeps the lone surrogate and writes it back as `\udXXX` (7 recorded).
  - Nesting of 128 or more levels is a `SettingsParseError` with serde's reason (`recursion limit exceeded …`) where V8 parses it; the file is never overwritten (4 recorded).
  - A number past f64 is `null`. That is the same when spread or written back (`JSON.stringify` prints `null`), but a `typeof` check differs.
  - Off Unix, `node_fs::describe` names only ENOENT/EPERM, and `node_fs::sys::mkdir_recursive` reports `create_dir_all`'s error on the requested path.
  - `start()`'s fable advisor clear writes synchronously; TS fires it and moves on.
  - The `model` / `advisorModel` clearers now use `shift_remove`, so JS `delete`'s key order is kept (D10a).
- Still open:
  - `trusted_cwd`'s workspace-root, additional-directory and `project_config_root_for` checks use Rust `trim` where TS uses JS `trim` (G03).
  - `mcp_config`'s private settings reader (G03) reads a top-level `1e400` file as `null` and throws a TypeError where TS finds no servers, and its parse reason is serde's, not sanitized (it only reaches the log); `settings::read_settings_js` would fix both.
  - Fs error text has one home since I14c (`node_fs`): `FsError` and `NodeError` share libuv's names, texts and one mkdir walk. Still open: an error without an OS errno (a NUL in a path) is the Rust error's text with no code as an `FsError`, and `UNKNOWN: unknown error` as a `NodeError`, where Node throws `ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE` before the call (`workspace_files` and `skills` check for NUL themselves).
  - `agents::agent_path` keeps a private `assertWithin` without the NUL refusal: a NUL-holding project cwd reaches the fs call (an fs error) where TS throws `null byte in path` (I14d left it; not on its list).

**JSON.parse (A14: `claudius_agent::js`, `claudius_server::body`)**
- A lone surrogate in V8's message (the token is half of an astral character, or the ±10-unit window cuts a pair) is dropped from the `String`. `json_parse_error_utf16` keeps it.
- `json_parse` / `JsonBody::parse_or_message` (and `js::parse_line`, I17: serde_json with `json_parse` as the fallback, so it reads the same): a lone-surrogate escape becomes U+FFFD; a number beyond `f64` range becomes `null` (`typeof` in JS still sees a number); nesting past serde_json's 127 levels is an `Err` with serde's text, where V8 parses any depth.
- The older `JsonBody` helpers (`parse` / `strict` / `lenient` / `or_bad_request`) strip one leading BOM where `Body.json()` strips two, and treat lone-surrogate escapes as malformed.

**Sessions store**
- `listSessions` (S33, `sessions_list.rs`) matches the SDK on 104 recorded calls (`tests/list_sessions_golden.rs`). The SDK hides no forks. It hides sessions continued in another (`continued-in`) and, in a `dir` listing, sessions whose cwd encodes to the listed dir's name but resolves elsewhere. Differences:
  - A lone-surrogate escape in a title, prompt or cwd becomes U+FFFD (JS keeps it). A line nested past 128 levels isn't read.
  - NFC, `\p{Cc}` / `\p{Cf}` and case mapping use the Rust crates' Unicode tables, not Node 22's ICU.
  - Not ported: the win32 `hE` drive-letter alias and the `sessionStore` / hover-rest / diskless paths, which the SDK host never takes. Windows isn't run. On Linux CI the NFC and worktree-case cases are skipped (macOS only), and the mode cases are skipped as root.
  - Untested: `git worktree list`'s 5 s timeout and 1 MiB stdout cap. The `nP` ancestry walk is unit-tested from the SDK source plus one golden case. Its 64-link cap can't show through a listing, because realpath hits ELOOP first and the session is kept.
  - `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME` and the NFC'd config dir apply only to the listing; `find_file` / `info` / `fork` and the JSONL watcher use the natural name. An empty `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` falls back to `~/.claude` (`paths::claude_config_dir`), where the SDK's `??` uses `""` (a relative `projects/`).
  - Candidates are read one at a time where the SDK reads up to 32 at once; the results are the same.
  - `/api/sessions/all` (D19) serves the listing. Not recorded: `list_all_indexed_sessions` reads the workspace list with `js_string` (I14a), so a lone-surrogate rootPath binds as TS binds it (`ED A0 80`) in `WHERE cwd = ?`; no golden has a lone-surrogate rootPath.
- `forkSession` (A04) matches the SDK on 33 recorded cases. Not ported: `sessionStore` forks. D05 serves `/api/sessions/fork` (`session_fork`).
- `getSessionMessages` (`transcript.rs`) matches SDK 0.3.312 on 13 recorded transcripts and 17 transcript files read through `sessions_store::messages` (I14b), with and without system messages (`tests/session_messages_golden.rs`): an origin keeps `taskKind` only as `"mcp-task"`, and the session-ended interrupt counts as a reply (R09).
- Fork route (D05):
  - A body nested past 127 levels is Next's empty 500 (V8 parses it). OPTIONS is axum's empty 405; Next answers 204.
  - A non-string `dir` throws darwin's `e.normalize is not a function` (recorded). The non-darwin `e.replace is not a function` comes from reading the SDK, not from a recording.
  - The fork lookup (`sessions_store::find_file_where`) doesn't NFC the `dir` on darwin. It also tries the unresolved `dir` after its realpath, where the SDK tries only the realpath.
  - The fork build is on SDK 0.3.312 (R09): `turnAttributionKey` is reset, and the last same-session `tools-used` trailer is forked, keeping its non-empty string `tools`.
  - `resolveTrustedCwd`'s worktree match still uses `sessions_store::git_worktrees`, not `parsePorcelain`. They differ only for a worktree path that ends in whitespace.
  - Mirrored TS bugs:
    - `createForkWorktree` throws after `git worktree add` when index.json's `forks` is a truthy primitive, or index.json is a directory. The worktree stays unrecorded and the route answers `worktree: null`.
    - An array `forks` silently drops the association.
    - With `worktree` set, a blank `cwd` resolves to the server's cwd.
- The messages and fork readers parse lines with serde. A line containing a lone-surrogate escape (`"\ud83d"`), which `JSON.parse` accepts, is skipped; in a fork that message isn't copied. The head/tail readers behind `info` and the listing fall back to `js::json_parse` (S33), so they read such a line, with U+FFFD for the lone surrogate.

**Managed settings (`managed_settings.rs`)**
- Read: the macOS MDM plist tier (A05b), the Windows registry (HKLM as the MDM tier, HKCU as the user-writable last resort; A05d), the WSL chain (A05e) and the managed-settings file tier (A05a), with the SDK's tier selection (ranks, fail-closed binding, `managedSourcesBehavior` merge). Matched against SDK 0.3.312 on 233 recorded cases, `policyOrigin` included, so a source holding none of the three keys must match too (R11).
- The registry and Linux cases were recorded on macOS against a fake `reg.exe`. Registry cases run the SDK's win32 branch. Linux cases emulate the OS facts, not the SDK's answers: `process.platform` reads "linux" in `$p()`, the loader and the platform class; `/proc/version` and the env come from the case; `xn` (the Windows folder under `/mnt/c`) is a temp dir. So the SDK's own `$p()` decides WSL, and `detect_wsl` must reach the same answer. There's no Windows or WSL CI, so a real `reg.exe`'s output is trusted to look like the captured samples.
- WSL: Linux with a non-empty `WSL_DISTRO_NAME` (ignored when `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` is exactly `remote_cowork` or `remote_cowork_trigger`, since 0.3.312) or a Microsoft/WSL `/proc/version`. The Windows policy (`/mnt/c` registry and `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode`) applies only once an admin source arms `wslInheritsWindowsSettings`. Even unarmed, a Windows source that fails to load shuts `/etc/claude-code` out, as in the SDK.
- Files are read like the SDK's `kje`: regular files only, up to 2 MiB, UTF-16LE after an `FF FE` BOM, else lossy UTF-8. One difference: a lone surrogate in a UTF-16 file becomes U+FFFD here, while Node keeps it in the string.
- The remote (server-managed) tier is never read, exactly like the SDK host (A05c). Only the CLI marks the cache eligible, and Claudius passes no `serverManagedSettings`. The fixture pins this with a seeded cache.
- Only `deniedModels`, `availableModelsMatch` and `modelPricing` are validated and returned, because they're the only keys Claudius reads from the managed source. The CLI applies its own full policy.
- Other keys are kept raw, because only their presence feeds the tier ranks. There's no Rust-side schema, so if the CLI would drop or substitute an invalid other key, it still counts as authored content here. That can let a tier outrank one the SDK would pick.
- On SDK 0.3.312 (R11). HKCU also loses the new admin-only `attributionProjectPolicy`. Not modelled: the rest of a cowork cloud host (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` `remote_cowork` / `remote_cowork_trigger` in the host's env). There the SDK asks the organization (`h_()`) and holds managed documents the process can write as restrict-only: `modelPricing` goes, `availableModelsMatch: "prefix"` is dropped, and tier selection weighs them as held. The registry read can also be withheld (`whereAsked`), though never on a real WSL host. Claudius doesn't run as a cowork cloud session; the recorder's control pair pins that the entrypoint is the gate.

**Workspaces (D01)**
- At parity on 423 of 425 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/workspaces.rs`). Differences: an icon id that leaves the icon dir (`..%2Fescape`) is refused (500, nothing written) where TS writes `.claudius/escape.png`; a number past f64 (`1e400`) is null, not Infinity (`rootPath: 1e400` → 400 "rootPath required" where TS 500s).
- Not recorded: JSON nested past 128 levels isn't parsed; an array-like icon `data` with length > 2 MiB gets the 400 without allocating; create reads the clock once (TS three times); one process-wide store mutex (TS interleaves); Next decodes dot segments in the path, axum doesn't; on 405s axum adds `Allow` and also answers OPTIONS with 405; invalid UTF-8 in a percent-decoded path segment is untested.
- `assertHomeIntegrity` (Node-only guard) is not ported.
- `sessions.rs` reads `claudius.workspace` with its own first-wins, undecoded `cookie()` helper rather than `workspaces::read_active_cookie`. Same result for real `wks_` ids; differs for a duplicated or percent-encoded cookie.
- `roots` uses only the `listWorkspaceRoots` slice; `workspace-roots.ts` is D02.

**Workspace relocation (G09)**
- At parity on every recorded case (69 module calls, 99 route requests). Differences:
  - The projects dir hangs off `paths::home_dir()`, so `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` moves it (TS: `os.homedir()`).
  - `fs.rm` removes a folder's children in parallel, so when several fail, which error Node reports is a race (usually the first in scandir order). Rust tries them all and reports the first in scandir order; the recorder keeps one failing child.
  - `close_db` drops the cached handle; a holder keeps a connection to the unlinked source file, where TS's `closeDb` closes the shared handle.
  - Not recorded: two relocations at once (TS interleaves at each `await`; Rust runs each on the blocking pool, the destination behind its DB mutex), copied files' timestamps / xattrs, sockets and devices in a sidecar.
- The `ATTACH` path is still bound `js_string::lossy`. Every other bind goes through `sql_text` since I15.

**SQLite text (I15)**
- At parity on every recorded case (18 binds, 39 byte sequences, 499 store calls) except the `KNOWN_*` ones:
  - A genuine U+10F800–U+10FFFF character is a `js_string` stand-in, so it binds as the lone surrogate it stands for (`ED A0 80`; TS `F4 8F A0 80`).
  - JSON TEXT read with `js::json_parse` (`sessions.state`, a notification's `payload`, a DB agent's definition; the open / pinned / active tabs read with `js_string::parse` since I14a) turns a `\udXXX` escape into U+FFFD, where `JSON.parse` keeps the surrogate. So the next `mergeSessionState` writes U+FFFD where TS writes the escape again.
- Not recorded:
  - JSON that Rust writes with `serde_json` (`prompt_drafts.images`, `queued_messages.images_json`, `session_usage.model_usage_json`) carries a stand-in as the 3-byte sequence; TS's `JSON.stringify` writes the escape. Unreachable today: those values arrive through `JsonBody` / `js::json_parse`.
  - A string that reached a store through `js::json_parse` or `JsonBody` already lost its lone surrogate (U+FFFD or refused), so it can't bind TS's bytes; only strings that keep stand-ins (`js_string::parse`) do.
  - `session_titles_golden`'s KNOWN legacy-title lone-surrogate entries remain (`read_legacy_title` parses with `js::json_parse`).

**Routes**
- Unread request bodies (I24): drained up to 1 MiB within 5 s, then the connection is cut (RST); Node reads any size for as long as the client sends. A request with `Expect: 100-continue` that gets an early answer gets no `100 Continue`, and the connection closes (Node sends `100 Continue` at once and reads the body). With `Connection: close`, Rust waits for the body and ends with a FIN, where Node closes after its answer and can reset a late body (the answer arrives either way).
- Agents (G05): match the TS on every recorded case. Differences: yaml nesting past 1000 levels fails the parse (V8 overflows somewhere past ~700–1700 levels), a lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD, a `Date` map key is named in UTC, a value nested past 2000 levels fails `JSON.stringify`; a body nested past 128 levels fails in serde (PUT `/api/agents` → empty 500, PUT `agents/db` → 400) where V8 parses; a string spelled exactly NUL+`js:Infinity` reads as Infinity. Mirrored TS quirks: `name: 1e400` → `Infinity.md`; a non-WAL `.claudius.db` lists `[]` until a write; a cyclic frontmatter gives an empty 500; PUT `agents/db` reloads no live session.
- Plugins (G04): match the TS on every recorded case (437 module cases, 274 requests). Mirrored: `String(pluginId)` keys (`1e400` → `Infinity`, an own-`toString` object → empty 500), `__proto__` adds nothing, `null` / primitive settings files give V8's TypeErrors (empty 500), a `null` `reload_plugins` reply → GET empty 500. Differences: a lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD; a file nested past 128 levels lists with `parseError` and refuses writes (422), and such a body is the empty 500, where V8 reads them; a top-level `-1e400` file's TypeError says `Infinity` (log only); an astral character in a string settings file spreads as two U+FFFD; a string spelled NUL+`js:Infinity` reads as Infinity. Not recordable: a CLI reply without `response` is `{}` (GET 200, reload `{}`) where TS gets `undefined` (GET 500).
- `memory/auto` / `memory/rules` (G08) match the TS handlers on 248 of 249 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/memory.rs`) and on every module call. Differences: a lone-surrogate escape in a PATCH body (`"\ud83d"`) reads as U+FFFD, so the response echoes the character where TS writes the escape (the file and index bytes are the same). The memory and user-rules dirs hang off `paths::home_dir()`, so `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` moves them (TS: `os.homedir()`).
- Models (G06): `GET /api/models`, `GET /api/models/probe` and `GET /:id/model` match the TS on every recorded request except these. A lone-surrogate escape in settings.json reads as U+FFFD (TS echoes `\ud800`). A settings.json nested past 128 levels doesn't load in serde_json (no curation), where V8 parses it. A probe CLI's error `result` without an `errors` array reports its `subtype` where the SDK's reader throws (see SDK host). Not recorded: the user settings path follows `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` (TS: `os.homedir()`). The probe runs on the sessions' CLI (`--claude-bin`), where the TS route lets the SDK find its own.
- Assets (D17) match the TS on every recorded step except 3 store steps the port refuses on purpose. Differences:
  - Deliberate: TS joins a row's hash into the store path, so a hash another writer stored with `..` reads and deletes files outside `assets/`. Rust treats those as missing (3 recorded steps).
  - `image-size`: a truncated image under 4 KiB decoded by `Buffer.from(…, "base64")` sits in Node's shared 8 KiB pool, so the package's `DataView` reads see the bytes after it. Rust treats them as past the end (no dimensions). Three loops in the package never end on a zero-size entry (HEIF `ispe`, JXL `jxlp`, ICNS) and grow an array until V8 runs out of memory; Rust gives no dimensions (5 recorded).
  - Past 63 rows the account scope's sort is a stable merge sort: V8's order unless a comparison is NaN (a foreign non-numeric `last_seen_ms` can cause one).
  - A send's image whose `data` is an array-like with a `length` past 2^31 - 1 is skipped (V8's allocation RangeError), where Node allocates it and reads every index. Not recorded (S29).
  - Untested: a `size_bytes` that isn't the file's length (a stale or foreign row) is sent as `Content-Length` as stored.
- `GET /model` applies the managed `deniedModels` policy from every managed tier the SDK host reads: macOS plist, Windows registry, WSL and the managed-settings files (A05). See Managed settings below.
- Every `/:id/*` route is ported (`account` by S30, 2026-11-01).
- `rewind` (S13) matches the TS handler byte for byte on 102 of 111 recorded cases (`tests/rewind.rs`). Differences: a body nested past 128 levels parses in V8 (a rewind) but not in serde_json (503 with serde's recursion-limit message). A lone-surrogate `userMessageId` (`"\ud800"`; the `lone surrogates` group, I20, 8 cases) gets the TS answer but reaches the CLI as U+FFFD's bytes where the SDK writes the escape back. Not recorded: a CLI reply without `response` gives `{"result":{}}` where TS gives `{}`.
- `resumeSessionAt` (S13): the create route drops a non-string one, where TS passes any truthy value to the CLI (`--resume-session-at=5`).
- `notification-prefs` (S24): matches the TS on every recorded case (143 route steps, 166 DB calls). Not mirrored: a body serde_json refuses but `JSON.parse` reads (a lone-surrogate escape, nesting past 128 levels) falls back to `{}` and patches nothing, where TS applies its `blocked` / `snoozeMinutes`. `is_session_muted` is read by the bus (`is_session_muted_with`, G01a), which sessions feed (S31).
- Usage extras (S16): the full `/context` body's `{ ...data, weeklyUsedSkills }` spread copies a string `data` by character where JS copies it by UTF-16 unit; they differ only for astral characters, and the CLI's `get_context_usage` reply is always an object.
- `mcp-dynamic` (S15a) matches the TS handler on 61 of 64 recorded cases (`tests/mcp_dynamic.rs`). Mirrored: a `null` config answers 503 with V8's `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'type')`, and a server named `__proto__` never reaches the CLI. Differences: a `{type: "sdk", instance: …}` entry is a plain config to Rust, while the SDK takes it for an in-process server, disconnects the session's in-process servers and throws (503 with a minified `t.instance.connect is not a function`). Not recordable: a CLI success reply without `response` gives `{"result":{}}` where TS gives `{}`.
- `mcp` (G03) matches the TS handlers on 416 of 423 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/mcp_config.rs`). Mirrored: `name in servers` finds prototype names (deleting `toString` rewrites the file and answers ok), array holes, the 500 on deleting `length`, a string `mcpServers` spread by UTF-16 unit, and `String(name)` keys (`1e400` becomes `"Infinity"`, an own-`toString` object is a 500). Differences: a lone surrogate (an escape in a file or body, or half of an astral character in a spread string) becomes U+FFFD where TS writes the escape back. A settings file or body nested past 128 levels doesn't parse (an empty 500) where V8 reads it. A malformed or non-UTF-8 percent-escape in `:name` is decoded by axum, where Next answers 400. `resolve_trusted_cwd` has no 5 s per-root `git worktree list` cache, which TS keeps. Not recordable: a CLI success reply without `response` leaves `status` out, where the SDK puts its TypeError in `statusError`.
- Schedule (G07b) matches the TS handlers on all 261 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/schedule_routes.rs`). Differences:
  - Served by Rust while it runs the scheduler (the default); with `--no-scheduler`, forwarded to Next. The Next behind it must run with `CLAUDIUS_RUST_SCHEDULER=1`.
  - Not recorded, from `json_parse`: a lone-surrogate escape in a stored field (`name`, `prompt`, `model`, `cron`) becomes U+FFFD; a body nested past 127 levels gets the empty 500 where V8 reads it.
  - A throw is logged as one `[api/<route>] <Error>: <message>` line at error level; Next prints the stack.
- `fs/dirs` / `fs/list` (D04) match the TS handlers on 297 of 298 recorded steps per platform (`claudius-server/tests/fs_routes.rs`). Differences:
  - A lone-surrogate escape in a POST `name` (`"\ud800"`) reads as U+FFFD (`JsonBody::parse_or_message`). The same folder is made (node also writes the name as UTF-8 with U+FFFD), but the body prints `"�"` where TS prints `"\ud800"`. A body nested past 127 levels is a 400 `invalid JSON`, where V8 parses it.
  - Entry order is ICU4X root collation, which is V8's order with no `LANG`. Next follows its process's ICU default locale, so under e.g. `LANG=sv_SE` it orders some names differently.
  - FS_ROOT's default and fs-list's `HOME` come from `paths::home_dir()`, so `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` moves them (TS: `os.homedir()`). FS_ROOT is read at the first request (TS: at module load).
  - Posix path semantics only (`node_path` is `path.posix`).
  - Not recordable: a `readdir` error other than `EACCES` (e.g. `EPERM` on a TCC-denied folder) answers Next's empty 500, like TS's rethrow; fs/list's `catch → 500` can't be reached.
- `heartbeat` / `heartbeatz` (D26a) match the TS on all 35 recorded requests. Differences:
  - TRACE is 400; Next answers 500 (`'TRACE' HTTP method is unsupported`).
  - Next's `rsc`-header 307 and trailing-slash 308 aren't mirrored.
  - HEAD may carry `content-length`.
  - `heartbeatz` probes the database of the Rust process's cwd. It's TS's only when both start in the same folder (`cargo run` from `rust/` probes `rust/`'s).
  - `/api/heartbeat` answers 200 from Rust while Next is down, so it no longer shows the UI is up behind the front door. Use the Next URL for that, as `playwright.rust.config.ts` does.
  - The other ported routes (all but `splash-examples`, D26b) don't send Next's `vary` header yet.
- Splash (D26a, D26b): the core modules match the TS on every recorded case, and `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/splash-examples` matches on 187 of 189 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/splash_examples_route.rs`). Differences:
  - A body nested past 127 levels is a 400 `invalid JSON` where V8 parses it (recorded).
  - Not recorded: a lone surrogate in an `accounts.json` label or `~/.claude.json`'s displayName reaches the fallback name as U+FFFD (the stores' `json_parse`), where TS keeps it; a real U+10F800–U+10FFFF character in a body string reads as a lone surrogate (`js_string`'s stand-ins); TS's handle cache is keyed `${mode}:${cwd}`, so a non-string rootPath whose `String()` names an open cwd reads and writes that DB, where Rust answers the defaults / 500; the no-workspace fallback is the Rust process's cwd.
  - TRACE, `rsc` 307 / trailing-slash 308 and HEAD's `content-length` as for heartbeat. DELETE runs its two deletes in turn (they fail together); PUT reads the chips once (TS twice). A throw is logged as one `[api/splash-examples] <message>` line.
- `bash` (`!` mode, S10) matches the TS handler on 39 recorded request bodies, run in order on one live session against a real shell with a fake `sudo` (`tests/bash.rs`). Remaining differences:
  - The shell inherits the server's environment. Next adds `NODE_ENV`, its `__NEXT_*` vars and the `.env*` files to `process.env`; Rust has only its own.
  - A spawn error other than `ENOENT` / `EACCES` / `EAGAIN` / `EMFILE` / `ENFILE` makes Node's `spawn()` throw (an empty 500); Rust reports it like those (`spawn /bin/bash <CODE>` in stderr).
  - Where the 16 Mi-unit cap cuts a surrogate pair, JS keeps a lone high surrogate. A Rust string can't, so that one unit is missing from the output; the count still matches.
- `transcript` / `search` (S14) match the TS handlers on 132 recorded cases (`tests/transcript_search.rs`). Remaining differences:
  - A transcript line holding a lone-surrogate escape (`"\ud800"`) is valid for `JSON.parse` but not for serde_json, so the shared reader drops that line; TS keeps it.
  - The SDK reads a transcript over 5 MiB only from its last `compact_boundary` on (unless `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_PRECOMPACT_SKIP`); Rust parses the whole file. A plain compacted 6 MiB transcript gives the same post-boundary chain either way (probed); preserved-segment shapes aren't checked.
  - Where the search loop throws, both answer an empty 500; Next logs the TypeError, Rust a generic line.
- `sessions/search` (D20) matches the TS handler on all 149 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/session_search.rs`). Differences:
  - With more matches than `limit`, TS answers the first `limit` reads to finish (8 at once, so it depends on file sizes and the thread pool); Rust answers the first `limit` in scan order (newest first). The golden pins TS to start order with `UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=1`.
  - A query of 32 768 or more UTF-16 units makes V8 throw `Regular expression too large` (an empty 500), but node's 16 KiB request-head limit answers 431 before such a URL reaches the handler. Rust takes it and searches.
  - `homedir()` is `paths::home_dir()`, so `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` moves the scan to the state home, where the CLI writes no transcripts. Parity e2e sets `HOME` and is unaffected.
  - Not recordable: a file over node's 2 GiB `readFile` limit, or whose text passes V8's 512 MiB string limit, is skipped in both (unit-tested with shrunk limits); names that aren't UTF-8 (APFS refuses them) are decoded lossily in both and dropped by the id check.
- `sessions/peer-source` (G11) matches the TS on all 174 recorded requests and 48 module calls (`claudius-server/tests/peer_source.rs`, `tests/peer_source_golden.rs`). Differences:
  - A registry record or transcript line nested past 1 000 levels is skipped (the `JSON.parse` port's depth limit), and a workspaces.json nested past 128 levels reads as no workspaces; V8 parses both.
  - OPTIONS is axum's empty 405; Next answers 204.
  - `homedir()` is `paths::home_dir()`, so `CLAUDIUS_STATE_HOME` moves `claudeDir` (as in D20). Parity e2e sets `HOME` and is unaffected.
  - Off unix, `live` is always `false`.
  - Not recordable: a line past V8's 512 Mi-unit string limit ends that file in both (unit-tested with a shrunk limit).
- `open-tabs` (D21) matches the TS on every recorded case except 3 (288 route requests, 18 concurrent, 134 DB calls): a body nested past 127 levels is a 400 `invalid JSON`, where V8 parses it and saves its tabs (the PUT and the row dump after it, and the module's 150-deep `lit` read). Lone surrogates match since I14a. Not recorded: TS keys its handle cache `${mode}:${cwd}`, so a non-string rootPath whose `String()` names an already-open cwd reads that cwd's strip; Rust treats every non-string cwd as a failed open. With no workspace or customization, the fallback cwd is the Rust process's own cwd (Next's is its repo root). A cookie that decodes to a genuine U+10F800–U+10FFFF character matches a store id holding the lone surrogate it stands for (`js_string`'s ambiguity, as on D01's workspace routes).
- Workspace git (D06) matches the TS handlers on 235 of 237 recorded requests (`tests/git_routes.rs`). Differences:
  - Deliberate: a `show` ref starting with `-` is refused the way git refuses an unknown option (500 `git show exited 128: fatal: unrecognized argument: <ref>:<path>`). TS passes it to git as an option: `--output=<file>` writes a file, `--format` prints HEAD, and `-S<x>` answers 200.
  - Not recorded: through the typed `workspaces::get`, a matched entry whose `rootPath` isn't a string reads as `""`, so git runs in the server's cwd (as TS does for a missing `rootPath`). TS's `execFile` throws `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE` on a non-string one, and each git helper handles that its own way.
  - git inherits the server's env; Next adds `NODE_ENV`, `__NEXT_*` and the `.env*` files.
  - A NUL-argument message cut through a surrogate pair drops the lone half that Node keeps.
  - A cwd that is a file reads `spawn git ENOENT`, where libuv says `spawn ENOTDIR`. Only `getRepoRoot` sees one, and it swallows both.
  - Where an overflow cuts output, the cut point and the killing signal (SIGTERM / SIGPIPE) depend on pipe timing, as in Node.
- Workspace branches (D07) match the TS handlers on 304 of 307 recorded requests (`tests/git_branches.rs`). Differences:
  - A lone-surrogate escape in a name (`"\ud800x"`) reaches Rust as U+FFFD, so `unknown branch: …` echoes that character where `JSON.stringify` writes the escape; git sees U+FFFD from both.
  - With a lone surrogate in the body, serde can't take the text, so the `1e400` stand-in is lost: `startPoint: 1e400` reads as no start point (`unknown branch`) instead of TS's Infinity (`startPoint must be a string`).
  - `JsonBody` stops at 128 levels of nesting; V8 parses any depth, so a 200-deep body reads as malformed (`name (string) required`).
  - D06's notes on git's env, NUL-message cuts and `spawn ENOTDIR` apply unchanged.
- Workspace commit (D08) matches the TS on all 161 recorded requests (`claudius-server/tests/git_commit.rs`). Differences:
  - The CLI env lacks the SDK's `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath=1`.
  - A diff cut at 200 000 units through a surrogate pair drops the lone half JS keeps.
  - Not recorded: an error `result` with no `errors` is `claude returned <subtype>`; the SDK reader throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')`.
  - Body nesting past 128 levels reads as malformed (D07's).
  - Deliberate, unreachable: remove checks each untracked path is inside the root before unlinking.
- Docker containers (D23) matches the TS handler on 112 of 114 recorded cases (`claudius-server/tests/docker.rs`). Differences:
  - A `docker` without a shebang runs through `/bin/sh` (std's `execvp` ENOEXEC fallback); Node's `spawn` throws `spawn ENOEXEC` synchronously, so TS answers `unknown` / `spawn ENOEXEC` without starting `stats`. Recorded.
  - An NDJSON line nested past 1000 levels is skipped; V8 parses it. Recorded.
  - Not recorded: a 4 MiB cut that falls inside a surrogate pair drops the high half JS keeps (only the overflowing stream's text, so only an `unknown` detail).
  - `docker` inherits the server's env; Next adds `NODE_ENV`, `__NEXT_*` and the `.env*` files.
  - Where a multi-byte stream overflows, the cut point depends on pipe read boundaries, as in Node.
- Feedback (G10): a lone-surrogate `comment` / `surface` (the `lone surrogates` group, I17) gets the TS answer but reaches the CLI as U+FFFD where TS forwards the escape, and is stored as U+FFFD's bytes where better-sqlite3 stores the surrogate's own three (`ED A0 80`; I15).
- Cost (D18b): a cost-cache entry whose mtime and size match but isn't Claudius-shaped is parsed again (TS uses it as is and may throw); day keys use the process zone through jiff (`TZ` first); a panicked overview run is a 500 `the cost overview task failed`.
- Updater (D27a): matches the TS on all 345 recorded requests. Differences:
  - `updater.json`'s read-modify-write is serialized in-process; TS lets two writers race.
  - The changelog fetch sends the client's default headers (lower-case names, `accept-encoding: identity`, no keep-alive; see `fetch.rs`), with `pragma` / `cache-control: no-cache` added for `cache: "no-store"`. A refused connection was replayed as a hang-up; both give `fetch failed`.
  - A fractional `?offset` is Next's empty 500, as TS's throwing `fh.read` gives, logged in Rust's words. The settings-unreadable warning prints the message, not the error object. OPTIONS is axum's 405 (Next: 204), not recorded.
  - Mirrored TS bugs:
    - an install root of `/` makes `log` answer 400 `invalid path` (`base + sep` is `//`);
    - a non-string `pending.remoteSha` reaches git as `String(v)` (one with its own `toString` as `""`, as Node's argv conversion does);
    - `constructor:` / `__proto__:` commits are classified, as truthy `Object.prototype` members, into no group, yet still throw on a bad `sha`;
    - an array `sha` gives an array `entry.sha`;
    - any non-null `html_url` becomes `compareUrl`;
    - the dedupe key is one string, so a `:` in a scope can collide.
- Updater (D27b): matches the TS on all 83 recorded applies and 20 scheduler cases. Differences:
  - The apply log's lines land in the order they are made. TS fires the `[install/…]`, `[build/…]` and `[claude] …` appends without awaiting them, so under load a later line can land first.
  - A restarter (`setsid` / `nohup`) that can't spawn is logged, and the self-SIGTERM still follows. TS has no `error` listener on that child, so it is an uncaught exception there (by inspection).
  - With the dev routes on, `CLAUDIUS_DEV_RESTART_LOG=<file>` records the restart (spawn plan, self-exit) instead of doing it.
  - The Claude merge / resolve sessions run on the server's `claude` (`--claude-bin`), not the SDK's bundled CLI; the argv and prompts match.
  - `spawnStreamed` decodes each pipe read on its own, as Node decodes each chunk; read boundaries can differ, so a multi-byte character split by one is replaced elsewhere (not recorded).
  - The scheduler's timers are tokio tasks with Node's clamp (a delay past 2^31 - 1 ms fires after 1 ms). `main.rs` boots it only with `--boot-tasks` (G12b).
  - Mirrored TS bugs: a recorded `conflicts` / `recovery` is read as the raw JS value (a numeric `fromSha` lets install and build run, then throws in `init`); a second apply gets the first one's outcome, whatever its `allowCcMerge`; the `merge`-phase rollback is dead code; a daemon restart without `bin/claudiusd` fails in `init` after `updater.json` already says `restarting`.
- Customization lifecycle (D14): matches the TS on all 74 recorded requests and 7 startup calls. Differences:
  - Deliberate: an id whose `<root>/<id>/src` isn't strictly inside the customizations root gets 403 `path outside allowed root` on sync, publish, auto-fix, description POST and build-app POST, as D13's `diff` does. TS walks, copies and deletes wherever `..` points.
  - The revert script and the app build run on the first `node` on PATH (then /opt/homebrew/bin, /usr/local/bin, /usr/bin, ~/.bun/bin/bun; with none, `spawn node ENOENT`). TS uses `process.execPath`.
  - A UTF-16 budget cut through a surrogate pair drops that unit; JS keeps the lone surrogate.
  - Not recorded:
    - the reconcile skipping a `kind:"customization"` workspace whose `id` isn't a string;
    - a root file named after an `Object.prototype` member and missing from the manifest, which reads `null` (TS: the inherited function);
    - the startup lines, which are tracing logs (the message, no stack).
  - Mirrored TS bug: the auto-fix prompt's `replaceAll` expands `$&` / `` $` `` / `$'` / `$$` in a conflicting path.
- Customization preview (D15): matches the TS on 120 of the 122 recorded requests. Differences:
  - Deliberate: the 403 for ids whose mirror escapes the root; the server's own port is never offered; starts and restarts are serialized; shutdown kills restart-replaced children too, with SIGKILL after 3 s; bun install doesn't block the server; the node dir always comes from the PATH scan; no Electron `resourcesPath` preview-runtime.
  - Not recorded: bun's 5-minute timeout and 1 MiB output cap; the sweep's ppid exemption; Linux probe semantics (the darwin group runs on macOS only); the `resourcesPath` branch; thrown spawn errnos other than ENOTDIR; TS's interleaved concurrent starts.
  - Mirrored TS bugs: killSquatters has no ownership check; a preview that ignores SIGTERM survives DELETE (the 5 s SIGKILL checks `child.killed`); a `[deps]` "Ready" line flips the status.
- The agent picked with `POST /:id/agent` lives in the session's state, but nothing rebuilds a session yet: TS carries it through `getRebuildOpts` into a recovered or restarted session (S19).
**Client state machine (U01)**
- So far: the pure helpers (U01a), the pure modules the reducer calls (U01b), the `ChatState` / `apply` / `Effect` frame and the events that don't touch the transcript (U01c), the frame switched to U01b's modules (U01m), and the `sdk` event's message and stream assembly: `assistant`, `stream_event`, `result` and `prompt_suggestion` (U01d), and the prompts and their answers (U01f), and the status, usage and mode events and actions (U01i), and user messages, tool results, tool progress, thinking estimates and the Edit / Bash / KillBash side effects (U01e), and the system entries, compaction notices, rate-limit pills, `session_snapshot` and history paging (U01h), and the tasks, todos and scheduled loops (U01g), and `moveToActiveAccount` / `effortChangeWouldWait` (U01n) and the queue mirror and send / queue actions (U01j), and the session lifecycle (U01k: boot, guard, create / switch / bind, the stream contract, recovery, visibility); every promise outcome (U01h's jump and U01g's todo edit included) is one `Effect::ActionResolved`. Still to come: the acceptance replay (U01l) and main's later `use-session.ts` changes (U01q). U01c's `clearPendingMessages` stays inline, because it isn't a U01b module. It ported these ahead of their slices: `replay_done` and the `/pending-prompts` merge (U01k), the 30 s poll and `refreshSessions` (U01k), and `requestRecap` / `dismissRecap` (U01j, which checked them: `requestRecap` now also rejects an empty session id).
- Not replayed (U01c):
  - `refreshSessions` with a non-numeric `lastModified` across 2 or more rows: V8 sorts treating the comparator's `NaN` as `0`, and the port's stable sort agrees only while the order stays consistent.
  - Object or array session ids, which the TS compares by reference.
  - A `null` entry in a `/pending-prompts` list: it throws inside a React updater and takes down the tree.
  - StrictMode: dev double-invokes updaters, so an MCP notice there takes two uuids.
- Not replayed (U01d): none left. U01g's scenarios now replay the subagent task-model stamp, U01i's scenarios now replay the effort wait dropped on a main-thread `message_start` and the model ref, U01e's a rebuild keeping a tool's result / `startedAt` and the thinking estimate, and U01h's the failed-compaction notice (`pendingCompact`) and `apiRetry` cleared by a reply or a result.
- Deliberate: these fields, which the TS copies unchecked, read as `""` when not a string: a tool_use `id` / `name`, a subagent `uuid`, a tool_result `tool_use_id`, and `tool_progress`'s ids, name and `subagent_retry` strings. These read as absent: a non-boolean `is_error`, a non-string `bashId` or `subagent_type`, a non-number `timedOutAfterMs` or `elapsed_time_seconds`, and a non-boolean `backgroundEndsWithFinalResponse`. A non-string `transcriptPath` reads as `null`. `ToolProgressInfo.parentToolUseId` and `SubagentRetry.errorStatus` are `null` when missing.
- Deliberate (U01b): a boot marker whose `at` is past f64 (`1e400`, JS `Infinity`) reads as no marker; a conversation-reset trigger named after an `Object.prototype` key reads "reason unspecified"; `enqueue_prompt` always replaces a queued `requestId` (the TS keeps the array only for the identical object). Not ported, because the hook doesn't call them: the task-status panel predicates, `describeApiRetry` / `humanizeApiRetryError`, `effortToastText`, `fastModeDisabledReasonLabel` and `currentPageLoad`.
- Deliberate (U01d / U01m): a non-string `uuid`, `parent_tool_use_id`, stream `message.id`, `error`, `resume_reason`, `startup_failure_reason` or `prompt_suggestion` reads as absent (`""` for an id); a usage count that isn't a number reads as 0 (the TS would concatenate a string); a `result` `errors` that isn't an array reads as none (the TS would index a string); a prompt's non-string `requestId` keys as `""`.
- Deliberate (U01f): with the `""` key, `5` and `{}` request ids collide in a queue, and a `prompt_settled` without an id also drops a queued prompt whose id is `null` or a number (the TS uses `===` and a `Set`). The server always sends a UUID string, and the replayed odd ids (missing, `7`, `null`) agree with the TS. Not replayed: the actions without a bound session (unit-tested).
- Deliberate (U01i): a shown effort level that isn't a string is held as `""` in force, so a repeated non-string `effort_applied` doesn't clear the "may wait" note (the TS `===` would). The action arguments are typed (`bool` toggles, `&str` levels). A non-string `subscriptionType` gives `rateLimitHit` no `subscriptionType` (U01d's string read; the TS copies it). Not replayed: the actions without a bound session (unit-tested).
- Deliberate (U01e): a user record's non-string `uuid` takes a random one (the TS keeps the number), a non-string `parent_tool_use_id` is the main thread (the TS keys a trail by it), a non-number `at` starts a background shell at now (the TS stores it as sent), and a non-string `user_message_uuid` stamps no thinking row. Not replayed: tool ids named after `Object.prototype` keys (the TS's `prev[id]` reads the prototype). U01g ported and replays a Task's result settling a real task, a result's CronCreate promotion, TaskCreate re-key and TaskList rebuild, and the TodoWrite / TaskCreate / TaskUpdate / Cron* / ScheduleWakeup side effects.
- Deliberate (U01h): a non-string `uuid` reads as `""`; a non-string `trigger` / `decision_reason_type` / `priority` / `timestamp` / reset `trigger` / typed `rate_limit_info` field reads as absent, `null` included (the TS shows `null`); a non-object `rate_limit_info` reads as none and only the `SDKRateLimitInfo` keys are kept; `api_retry`'s non-number `error_status` reads as `null`, a non-string `error` as `""`; snapshot lists keep only their strings, and a `lastUserPrompt` with a non-string `uuid` / `text` isn't injected. `jumpToUuid`'s outcome is a dedicated `Effect::JumpResolved`.
- Not replayed (U01h), each unit-tested: `loadOlder` with a page in flight (`act()` answers every fetch within its step), the URIError on a lone-surrogate head uuid, the odd fields above, and reset triggers `null` / `5`.
- Fixed in the TS (U01o): `jumpToUuid` read `messagesRef` / `hasMoreAboveRef`, which effects sync only after React commits. That happens a macrotask after the `await loadOlder()` continuation, so the hook re-fetched the first cursor and resolved `null` for a target two pages up. It now resolves against the pages it merged, which is what the port always did, and the golden replays it.
- Deliberate (U01g): a non-string `task_id` / `taskId` keys as `""` (the TS keys `"undefined"` / `"5"`), and a non-string notification `status` is `""`; the other typed task fields read as absent, and `resource_links` keep only `TaskResourceLink`'s keys; a non-number tool `at` arms a loop at now. Where the TS throws inside a React updater (taking the tree down), the port changes nothing: a non-object `task_updated` `patch`, a non-object snapshot entry (skipped), a non-array `innerMessages` (none). `updateTodoItem`'s outcome is a dedicated `Effect::TodoItemUpdated`, and the todo actions' warnings are `Effect::ConsoleWarn`.
- Not replayed (U01g), each unit-tested: `task_snapshot`'s orphan hunt paging back (under `act()` it spins, like `jumpToUuid`), a page in flight, a failed page and the 200-page cap; the throwing payloads and odd field types above; the todo actions without a session.
- TS bug found (U01g), not ported (by inspection): `loadUntilTasksFound` reads `messagesRef` / `hasMoreAboveRef` / `loadOlderRef` after `await loadOlder()`, the same stale-ref race `jumpToUuid` had before U01o. The port looks at each page once it is folded in. TS fix pending (TODO U01r).
- Deliberate (U01n / U01j): promise outcomes are JSON values in the TS's shapes, and two in-flight calls of one action resolve in fetch-settle order (no call id). The action arguments are typed: `SendImage` (a non-string image id can't be passed; the TS would keep it), `reorder_queued(dir: f64)` and `FeedbackInput`. A 503 send `error` object that shadows `toString` reads as `""` (the TS throws inside a React updater). Not replayed, each unit-tested: every action without a bound session (`None` / `""`), an SSE title echo landing during a rename, and an `editQueued` images object with a `length`.
- Deliberate (U01k): a create reply whose `id` isn't a string binds its JS string (`5` → "5", missing → "undefined"); badge seeds keep only string uuids; the visibility check reads the readyState the host last reported; after `unmount` the stream callbacks and visibility do nothing; `SystemEnv` has no storage, so under it the boot guard never skips (a host supplies `session_storage`); `location` is a mirror the host updates with `set_location`; `refreshSessions` is `refresh_session_list`; `streams` keeps a small entry per bind for the stale-callback guard.
- Mirrored (U01k): a late message from a replaced socket of the same session still applies; switch and create keep the badge timer and `streamDownSince`; a failed wake POST still binds; a skipped session's marker survives until that session replays; every query edit re-serialises the whole query (`%20` → `+`, a lone `?` dropped); the URL writer reads the live URL when it fires and writes only on a lowercase-hex chat root; `""` counts as unbound; after 5 s with no session the `?prompt=` seed strips the URL and posts nothing.
- Not replayed (U01k): StrictMode's double mount. `DRIFT` (4 steps) shows main's later `use-session.ts` changes until U01q.

**Transport (U02)**
- Not emulated: redirects (a 3xx fails a stream; no route redirects), `https://`, Chromium's charset refusal, the 6-connection limit, CORS. A `Last-Event-ID` with control bytes no header can carry fails the source.
- In-process responses have no `date` or `transfer-encoding`. A body that fails early: over HTTP it can be a network error with no head; in-process the head arrives, then the body errors. In-process, a handler runs to completion if the caller drops before the head.
- Deliberate: lowercase `patch` is sent as given (the server answers 405, as `fetch` does); `Last-Event-ID` goes as UTF-8 bytes (Chromium; undici refuses non-ByteString ids); the id buffer starts from the last id after a reconnect; `retry:` applies when read (Chromium; undici at the block's end); `ready_state()` moves when the host takes the event; dropping an `EventSource` closes it.
- Remote access has no authentication (U02e, for the user).

**Native UI theme (U03b–U03d)**
- Fonts: Geist / Geist Mono are bundled (U03c), without italics. The React UI's `italic` / `<em>` is Chromium's synthesized oblique; gpui doesn't synthesize one, so it draws the upright face. A theme file has no font features, so gpui-component's own code surfaces (TextView, code editor, diff) take only the family; the ligature-off features come from `fonts::code_style` where U04 applies them. Geist Mono has no `liga` / `calt` today, so nothing differs on screen (globals.css's comment that it "ships programming ligatures" is stale; TS, the user's call).
- Icons: the catalog's Lucide 1.43 drawings, except the 5 Lucide redrew since lucide-react 1.14 (`book-open`, `calendar`, `calendar-clock`, `circle-check`, `zap`), which are vendored from lucide-react (U03d). A lucide-react bump needs `rust/scripts/lucide-icons.mjs` rerun; the drift test fails until then.
- Code: gpui colors highlight names, not scopes. `storage.type` / `support.type` take `entity.name.type`'s color, `variable.parameter` is #e6edf3, Markdown bullets are `punctuation`, and the link underline, descendant rules and diff backgrounds aren't drawn.
- `selection` is capped at 30% alpha by gpui's theme-file loader. `apply` restores 35% / 40%, but a bare `Theme::change` shows the capped value.
- `ThemeColor`'s role fields are a best fit to React usage. React has no `:active` states, no solid info / success / warning buttons, no switch and no candlestick charts. Components (U04+) take their exact classes from their React counterparts.
- Upstream: the syntect build of gpui-component drops `editor.*` and `comment.doc` from theme files without an error, so the crate sets the style by field.

**Native transcript (U05a)**
- Native-only: rows over 4 KB fold (U00a), and a reply that streamed in stays open until Show less. Stick-to-bottom re-arms within 1 px of the bottom (GPUI), not the TS's 80 px `NEAR_BOTTOM_PX`.
- Not yet (U05c): the sticky pinned prompt (rows carry `pinned`), hover timestamps (ultra-verbose only), image thumbnails (text tokens), `notificationColor` on peer rows, and the rate-limit pill's detail lines. The markdown pre-passes are also missing.
- Elsewhere: Copy / Rewind / Restore are U07.
- Math (U05b): `$$` blocks render as code blocks of raw TeX. Inline `$…$` stays literal text, where the TS promotes it to math. User prompts follow `<Markdown breaks math={false}>`: `$` is escaped and soft breaks become hard.
- Mirrored TS quirks:
  - pinning ties go to the later prompt, and `<task-notification>` prompts never pin;
  - "Beginning of conversation" shows only above 20 messages;
  - the rate-limit threshold uses `>=`;
  - the working row hides while a question waits;
  - compact verbose hides the status ticker and reminders;
  - switching to or from ultra-verbose forgets the cards' clicks.
- Cost: markdown is parsed twice when a row is created (gpui-component re-applies `raw_html(false)` at first layout). The memo keeps a copy of each message. At 10k rows a stream delta costs 5–17 ms CPU (U05d).

**Native tool cards (U06)**
- Gaps:
  - "Not run · rejected" never shows: `ToolResult` has no `rejected` (U06b).
  - The HTML preview is a note until U12's WebView (U06c).
  - No pulse (U06d).
  - The host wiring (file links, settings, actions) is U06a.
- Deliberate differences:
  - **Layout:** every `<pre>` wraps (the TS scrolls the ToolCall input / result, JSON and the Workflow script sideways). Folding past 4 KB is native. Card margins approximate CSS margin collapsing (8 px between cards).
  - **Display:** running dots are static, and `title=` attributes are tooltips. The dev-only `tool_use_id` line isn't drawn.
  - **Verbose:** flipping the verbose level clears every `tool:` toggle.
- Mirrored TS quirks:
  - "1 tools" in the Task and Workflow stats.
  - Editor URLs are `vscode://file//abs/path`.
  - JSON key tokens include their colon.
  - The running timer ticks on while the stream reconnects.
  - A top-level call the user stopped shows as an error card.
  - An unknown schedule action shows as typed.
  - On light themes, chips keep the TS's dark-tuned amber / sky / emerald shades.

**Native sessions browser (U11)**
- The one-session transcript is plain text (U11c), and the data comes from the fixture until U11b.
- Deliberate differences:
  - **Native-only:** keyboard selection (↑ ↓ Home End Enter), and ⌘F → `nav.findSession`.
  - **Export and delete:** export opens a save dialog under ~/Downloads and says "Exported to …" or "Export failed: …". Delete confirms in a dialog over the screen area, not `confirm()`.
  - **Errors:** a fork that fails in transport says "Fork failed", where the TS leaves an unhandled rejection. The JSON parse error text differs from the browser's.
  - **Data:** the session page skips the TS's unused global listing fetch, and a listing row that isn't an object is skipped. Unread counts come from `SessionsData::unread`.
  - **Formatting:** `toLocaleString` uses Node 22's plain space before AM/PM. Compaction stats assume integer token counts.
- Mirrored TS quirks:
  - `n:` is sent raw to the transcript search.
  - Branch chips count trimmed names but filter on the untrimmed branch.
  - The rename draft isn't reset on cancel, and the rename box pre-fills `claudiusTitle ?? customTitle ?? ""`.
  - The unread filter stays latched.
  - "Load more" hides during its own fetch.
  - A 200 with `error` set keeps the old list.
  - A failed listing reads `HTTP <status>` without looking at the body.

**Native prompt cards (U08a)**
- Gaps:
  - The AskUserQuestion HTML preview shows as text (U08b).
  - Over a live chat the auto-mode tip ignores `disableAutoMode` (U08f).
  - No paste routing to the ask outside a field, no file drops (U08e).
- Deliberate differences: the cards dim the chat pane, not the window. The rest is in TODO U08a.

**Native live shell (U04b)**
- Gaps:
  - Remote mode is unauthenticated (U02e).
  - Session storage is per mount (U04f).
- Deliberate differences:
  - **Engine:** the scheduler is opt-in (`CLAUDIUS_APP_SCHEDULER`); the updater and boot tasks never run; there is no Next proxy or listener.
  - **Workspace cookies:** set client-side, per workspace.
  - **Events:** window activation stands in for focus, and GPUI window visibility for `document.hidden`.
  - **Icons:** cached by extension + `updatedAt`, not by HTTP max-age.
  - **Snapshots:** throttled to 16 ms; the bound tab reads "starting" while a switch is in flight.
  - **Navigation:** the chat's `ReplaceUrl` replaces the route; `AssignUrl` remounts the chat.
  - **Storage:** localStorage is a JSON file (`~/.claude/.claudius/native-app/local-storage.json`).
- Mirrored TS quirks:
  - SideNav: a non-ok `/api/customizations` keeps the gated tiles hidden; a fetch error means an empty set.
  - A legacy focus value `"1"` reads as focus.
  - Route memory's legacy values and its `/^\/wks_[a-f0-9]+(\/.*)?$/` rule.
  - `newTabId` is `tab-` + 16 hex digits.
  - The StatusLine and MessageList texts are copied as is.

**Native app shell (U04a)**
- Every screen but chat is a placeholder naming its route and parameters until its U-task.
- Native back/forward buttons and ⌘[ / ⌘] stand in for the browser's history; ⌘⌥⇧L and a title-bar button cycle system → light → dark (new). Close-all asks in an in-window dialog, not `confirm()`.
- No unread badges (U15), drawers (U15/U17), drag reorder / resize or pulse / glyph animations (U04c). `nav.toggleSidebar` (⌘B) isn't bound: the TS has no handler.
- Mirrored TS quirks: the overflow fit subtracts TRAILER_PX from a width that already excludes the chevron and close-all (~120 px stay empty); close-all leaves the bound session with no active tab; re-binding the same id after closing the last tab doesn't re-add it (`lastAddedKey`); tab shortcuts and ⌘. act only on the chat; route memory drops the query; `?x=` survives R1/R2 but not R4; `files?path=` encodes `/` as `%2F`; the overflow menu's X and pin keep it open.
- Icons: 182 names / 177 Lucide icons since the main merge dropped `Link` from `StatusLine.tsx`.

- The rest of `app/api` (182 route files in total): see the phases below.

## Phases

The step-by-step work queue (every route, session feature, UI screen and
release task, with acceptance criteria) is **[TODO.md](TODO.md)**.


| Phase | Scope | State |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Workspace, agent SDK host, fake CLI | ✅ |
| 1 | Session engine + manager, stores, core session routes, SSE, proxy | ✅ live-verified |
| 2 | Remaining `/api/sessions/:id/*` routes (list above) and their session features (rewind, account per session; effort, fast mode, output style, todos, background tasks, bash mode, transcript search and recap are done) | next |
| 3 | Session-aware global routes, so nothing degrades: `mcp`, `plugins`, `agents`, `models`, `schedule` (+ the scheduler itself), `memory`, `notifications` (+ bus), `accounts`/`account`, `workspaces/*/move-sessions`, `feedback` | |
| 4 | Disk-only routes, so Next serves only the UI: `workspaces`, `settings`, `customize*`, `customizations`, `skills`, `hooks`, `keybindings`, `fs`, `assets`, `local-image`, `cost`, `limits`, `worktrees`, `claudemd`, `doctor`, `docker`, `community`, `voice`, `updater`, `heartbeat*`, `heapdump`, `splash-examples`, the `sessions/*` collection routes | |
| 5 | Serve the UI without a Node server: build the React UI as static assets and serve them from axum (needs the server components / route handlers audited), then retire Next | |
| 6 | Native Rust UI on top of `claudius-core`, replacing React and the Electron shell. Toolkit decided 2026-11-16: GPUI + gpui-component (TODO U00, [docs/adr-ui-toolkit.md](docs/adr-ui-toolkit.md)); confirmed by the U00a spike on 2026-11-17 (no must-have failed; rows over ~4 KB must be folded; manual IME / VoiceOver checks pending, U00b) | toolkit decided, spike done, tokens ported (U03a) |

Phases 2–4 can run in parallel route by route. Each step: port the handler,
cover it in `tests/http.rs` (or a new test file), run the relevant Playwright
specs against `:3100`, and remove it from the gap list above.

## Parity rules (read before porting a route)

- **Same status codes, error strings and JSON shapes** as the TS handler. The UI and Playwright specs depend on them.
- **Dev-only routes** (TS: `if (NODE_ENV === "production") return …`) start with `tri!(dev_only(app.dev_routes, DevRefusal::…))`, before params, the session lookup or the body. Pick the refusal the TS handler uses (`NotFound` → 404 `dev-only endpoint`, `Forbidden` → 403 `dev only`).
- **Request bodies: copy how the TS line reads them.** Use `claudius_server::body`, and call it at the same point in the handler (TS checks the session and returns 404 *before* `req.json()` runs):
  - bare `await req.json()` → `tri!(JsonBody::strict(&body))`. An empty or malformed body gets Next's response to a thrown handler, an **empty** 500 with no content type, not a JSON error.
  - `.catch(() => ({}))` / `.catch(() => null)` / a `try/catch` that assigns a fallback → `JsonBody::lenient(&body, fallback)`.
  - the same, in a handler that only tells numbers apart with `Number.isFinite` → `JsonBody::lenient_overflow(&body, fallback, &stand_in)`: a literal past f64 (`1e400`, ±Infinity in JS) reads as the stand-in instead of failing the whole body.
  - a `try/catch` returning `400 {error}` → `tri!(JsonBody::or_bad_request(&body, "msg"))`.
  - a `try/catch` that uses the error's `message` (`rewind`'s 503, `settings/import`'s `invalid JSON: …`) → `JsonBody::parse_or_message(&body)`. Its `Err` is V8's `JSON.parse` message byte for byte (`claudius_agent::js::json_parse_error`). It strips two leading BOMs like `Body.json()`, and reads lone-surrogate escapes as U+FFFD.
  - the same, in a handler that stores or echoes the body's strings → `JsonBody::parse_js_overflow(&body, &stand_in)` (I14a): lone surrogates kept as `js_string` stand-ins; answer with `js_string::stringify` (`sessions/open-tabs`, `splash-examples`).
  - Afterwards, `body?.x` is plain indexing (`b["x"]`; `Null` for missing / non-object). `body.x` without `?.` throws on a `null` body, so use `tri!(js_member(&b, "x"))`, whose `Ok(None)` means `undefined`.
  - Any JSON value is a valid body (`null`, arrays, strings), exactly like `JSON.parse`.
- **JS numbers.** `serde_json` uses `preserve_order` + `float_roundtrip`. Write integral `f64` values as integers with `claudius_core::js_num`, because JS prints `3`, not `3.0`.
- **Re-serialized JSON** (TS `JSON.stringify` of parsed or merged data) goes through `claudius_agent::js::stringify`, not `serde_json::to_string`. serde keeps the source number text (`1.0`, `1e2`) and insertion order. JS prints `1` and `100`, formats like `1e+21`, and enumerates array-index keys (`"2"`, `"10"`) first. `claudius_agent::js` also has JS `truthy`, `trim`, `entries`, `prop` (`?.`), `Set` dedupe, and `json_parse` / `json_parse_error` (`JSON.parse` and the exact wording of V8's `SyntaxError`, replayed against node 22 in `tests/json_parse_golden.rs`). Where a parsed string is stored or echoed and may hold a lone surrogate, use `claudius_core::js_string::{parse, stringify}`: the same functions, with lone surrogates kept as stand-ins (`js` reads them as U+FFFD). These are the only two JS layers, because `claudius-agent` can't depend on `claudius-core` (I14c). With `preserve_order`, `Map::remove` is `swap_remove`: use `shift_remove` for JS `delete`.
- **JS string lengths are UTF-16 units.** Use `encode_utf16().count()` for caps and slicing, e.g. draft text caps and project dir encoding.
